--- Page 238 ---
<meta>This page continues the nightingale’s argument regarding human diversity. It lists various religious sects and denominations to prove that while humans share one physical form, their beliefs and minds are incredibly fractured and inconsistent compared to animals.</meta>

<page-num>220</page-num>

<header>->**THE BRETHREN OF PURITY**<- <note>original: "IKHWANU-S-SAFA"</note></header>

'Abālīs. <note>The 'Abālīs were likely a specific sect mentioned in the context of the previous page's list.</note> The **Goliathites** are followers (according to Muslim opinion) of the giant whom David killed. How they came to believe that Goliath was the leader of a sect—and a sect of *Jews* above all—remains a mystery! 

Among the Christians, there are Nestorians, Jacobites, and Malekāīs.¹ Among the Fire-worshippers <term>Zoroastrians</term>, there are Zoroastrians, Zarwanites,² Hurmīs, Mazkīs, Bahrāmīs, and Mānawīs <note>Manichaeans</note>. Among the Muslims, there are Shīa's, Sunnīs,³ Khārijīs, Rāfizīs, Nāsibīs, Murjīs, Kādarīs, Jahimīs, Mu'tazilīs, and Asha'rīs.

¹ Any good dictionary will provide information on the Nestorians and the Jacobites. The *Malakāīs* are a sect that is half-Christian and half-Muslim, named after their founder. They hold the same opinions concerning Christ that Muslims do.

² I regret that I cannot provide further information <note>original: "throw no light"</note> on the specific beliefs <term>tenets</term> of the Zarwanites and the other sects of the Fire-worshippers.

³ The **Sunnīs** are the orthodox Muslims who follow the traditional portion of Islamic law, the *sunnah* <note>original: "sunnat"</note>. This tradition was gathered from the words and actions of the prophet Muhammad and carefully preserved by his companions. They also equally respect the Caliphs <term>Khalifas</term> Abū Bakr, 'Omar, 'Osmān, and 'Alī. 

The **Shīa'hs**, on the other hand, regard 'Alī alone as the true Leader <term>Imām</term> and Successor <term>Khalīfa</term> after Muhammad; they view the other three as usurpers. They hold the companions of the prophet in contempt and pay very little attention to the traditions preserved by them. 

The **Kharijīs** refuse to give the title of Caliph or Imam to either Osmān or 'Alī. They were given this name because they "went out" <note>original: "went forth"</note> from 'Alī’s leadership after the battle of Siffīn. 

The **Nāsibīs** (from the word for "enmity" <note>original: "Nasb"</note>) were opposed to 'Alī and his claims to the Imamate and the Caliphate. 

The **Mu'tazilīs** are a rationalist sect among the Muslims. They deny that God possesses distinct physical or human attributes, declaring that perfect unity consists in the total absence of such attributes. 

The **Rāfizīs** are a sect of the Shī'ahs; they are called "The Deserters" because they abandoned Zeyd (the son of 'Alī, the son of Hosain, the son of 'Alī) when he forbade them from speaking against the companions of the prophet. 

The **Murjīs** are a sect of Muslim Antinomians <note>those who believe faith releases them from the obligation of following moral laws</note> who claim that faith consists only of words without deeds; they argue that disobedience does not harm a person who has faith, and that obedience is useless without faith. 

The **Kādarīs** are a branch of the Mu'tazilīs; they believe that man is a completely free agent with total free will. 

The **Jahimīs** are the exact opposite of the Kādarīs; they believe that man has no power at all and that what we call "voluntary actions" are actually just natural tremors. They even claim that God forces men to commit sins. 

The **Asha'rīs** are a sect of Sunnīs who differ only slightly from the mainstream orthodox group.

<summary>This page catalogs the vast array of religious sects and theological divisions within Christianity, Zoroastrianism, and Islam. It serves as evidence in the animals' argument that human thought is deeply divided despite their uniform physical appearance.</summary>

<keywords>religious sects, Sunnis, Shias, Mu'tazilites, Kharijites, theology, Christian denominations, Islamic history, Brethren of Purity</keywords>

andria, Cyrene, Spain, Constantinople, Azerbaijan,¹ Armenia, Syria, Greece, Iraq,² Badakhshan, Gurgan, Gilan, Nishapur, Karman, Kabul, Multan, Khorasan, Transoxiana,³ Khwarezm,⁴ Fergana, and others—thousands of cities and countries which are too numerous to be counted. God (may He be exalted) causes the sustenance of everyone to reach them and keeps them under His protection. Now this vast number, diversity of circumstances, and variety of occupations and aims all point to this: namely, that man is superior to creatures of a different order and takes precedence over all other classes of the animal creation; thus it is perceived that men are the masters and that all animals are their slaves. In addition to these, we possess other superior qualities, the description of which would be very long.”

<note>¹ Azerbaijan is a province of Persia, of which Tabriz is the capital, corresponding with the ancient Media.</note>

The frog said to the king: “This man has mentioned the vast numbers of mankind and boasts of it. If he were to see the aquatic animals and behold their shapes and forms, men would appear extremely few in his eyes; the cities and countries he mentioned would also be seen as comparatively few. For in the habitable fourth part of the earth, there are fourteen great seas: the Mediterranean Sea, the Sea of Jurjān <note>The Caspian Sea</note>, the Red Sea, the Persian Gulf, the Sea of Sindh, the Indian Ocean, the China Sea, the Sea of Aral, the Western Ocean, the Eastern Ocean, the Northern Ocean, the Southern Ocean, the Ethiopic Sea, and the Afric Ocean. 

And there are five hundred small rivers and two hundred large rivers, such as the Oxus, the Tigris, the Euphrates, the Nile, and others—the length of each varies from two hundred to two thousand miles. As for the rest—the smaller and larger streams, water-courses, ponds, and reservoirs in jungles and wildernesses—they cannot be counted. They are inhabited by thousands of kinds of aquatic animals, such as fish, tortoises, crocodiles, porpoises, alligators, and others, which none but the most High God knows and can count. Some say there are seven hundred classes of aquatic animals, not including species and individuals; and that the dwellers on dry land—the wild animals, beasts of prey, quadrupeds, and so on—consist of only five hundred classes, not

# CHAPTER XXV.

## CONTAINING AN ACCOUNT OF THE WORLD OF SPIRITS.

WHEN the frog finished this speech, a sage of the Genii said: “O you assembly of men and animals! You are ignorant of the multitudes of such of God’s creatures as are spirits, composed of light, and totally unconnected with material bodies:—who are pure intelligences, simple essences, and abide in the celestial orbs. Some of these—those who form the band of angels—are stationed in the vaults of heaven; and some, who dwell in the space filled by the atmosphere, are the Genii, and the band of evil spirits. Now, if you possessed any knowledge of the vast numbers of these creatures, you would know that men and animals are as nothing compared with them. For the space of the atmosphere is ten times that of the land and water; and the space of the ethereal <note>In so translating *kura-i-falak*, I am guided by the Arabic, which has, “*kuratu’l aṣīr-al aṣīr* (the ether)”—is the ethereal sphere; the Urdu should, I think, have been *kurā-i-ātish*.</note> sphere again is ten times that of the atmosphere. ---

<note>Muslims believe in the examination of the dead in the tomb by two angels named *Munkir* and *Nakīr*, who have a terrifying appearance. They will cause the body (to which the soul shall be temporarily reunited) to sit upright in the grave and will question the deceased regarding their faith. They will severely torture the wicked, but they will not hurt the good. —Lane's Modern Egyptians, chapter iii.</note>
<note>The words in the Arabic are "*an nairān*," meaning "the fires." Hell, like heaven and earth, is supposed by Muslims to consist of seven levels, one beneath another, with each being hotter than the one above it. *Jahannam* is both the general name for Hell and the specific name for its first level. *Jahīm* is the second level, and so on, down to *hāwīya*, the lowest hell.</note>
<note>See the <term>Korān</term>; chapter lxix. (The Inevitable) verse 36.</note>
<note>*Zakūm* is a tree at the bottom of Hell, whose fruit is like the heads of devils or terrifying serpents.</note>

## A COURSE OF MATHEMATICS, Pure and Mixed,

More especially designed for the use of candidates for the military and civil service examinations; with a variety of problems in, and practical applications of, the following subjects:—

1. ALGEBRA, TRIGONOMETRY, and MENSURATION.
2. ANALYTICAL GEOMETRY (CONIC SECTIONS), STATICS, DYNAMICS, and HYDROSTATICS.
3. THE DIFFERENTIAL and INTEGRAL CALCULUS, with their applications to higher geometry, mechanics, etc.

-> **By J. R. YOUNG,** <-

-> FORMER PROFESSOR OF MATHEMATICS, BELFAST COLLEGE. <-

---

-> LONDON:—WM. H. ALLEN & Co., 13, WATERLOO PLACE, S.W. <-
